MINISTRY OF PERSONNEL, P.G. & PENSIONS 
(Department of Pension & P.W.) 

New Delhi, the 18th January, 1993 

S.O. 258.—In exercise of the powers conferred by the pro¬ 
viso to article 309, read with clause (5) of article 148, of 
the Constitution and after consultation with the Convporoller 
and Auditor General of India in relation to persons serving 
in the Indian Audit and Accounts Department, the President 
hereby mates the following rules further to amend the 
Central Civil Services (Pension) Rules, namely 

1. (1) Theso rules may be called the Central Civil Services 
(Pension) First Amendment Rules, 1993. 



[*n»r II—3 (ii)]

(2) They shall come into force on the date of their pub¬ 
lication in the Official Gazette. 

2. In rule 54 of the Central Civil Services (Pension) Rules, 
1972. in sub-rale (14), in clause (b), in sub-clause (ii), the 
words “before retirement" shall be omitted. 

[No. U66)-P&PW/89-E]

New Delhi, the 18th January, 1993 

S.O. 251.—In exercise of the powers conferred by the 
proviso to article 309 road with clause (5) of article 148 of 
the Constitution and after consultation with the Comptroller 
& Auditor General in relation to persons serving in the ffidian 
Audit and Accounts Depattment, the President hereby makes 
the following rules further to amend the Contributory Provi¬ 
dent Fund Rules (India), 1962, namely :— 

1. (1) These rules may be called the Contributory Provi¬ 
dent Fund (India) Amendment Rules, 1993. 

(2) They shall bo deemed to have come into force from 
the 1st March, 1992. 

2. In rule 8 of the Contributory Provident Fund Rules 
(India), 1962, in sub-rule (l) for clause (b), the following 
clause shall be substituted, namely :— 

“(b) It may be any sum, so expressed not less than ten 
per cent of the emoluments and not more than his 
emoluments." 

[No. 20(15)-P&PW/90-E] 
SWARAN DASS, Dy. Secy. 

EXPLANATORY MEMORANDUM 

In the light of revision of employee’s subscription/emp¬ 
loyer’s share of contribution to the Employees Provident Fund 
from 8-1/3 per cent to 10 per cent the Government of India 
have also decided to enhance the emp'ovees subxiiption] 
employer’s share of contribution to the CPF from the exist¬ 
ing 8-113 per cent to 10 per cent w.e.f. 1-3-1992. It is certified 
that none of the employees governed by these rules will be 
adversely affected with this amendment. 

NOTE 

The second edition of the Contributory Provident Fund 
(India), Rules 1962 (corrected upto 16-2-87) hns since been 
brought out in diglot form. The rules have, subsequently been 
amended vide the notifications mentioned below :— 

1. F. 20(1 l)-P&PWl86-E dated 9-11-90 

2. F. 13(5)-P&PW/90-E dated 27-11-90 

3. F. 13(4)-P&PW/90-E dated 3-12-90 

4. F. 2(X26)-PAPW!88-E dated 2-5-1991.

for Assessment Year 1992-93, the specified date shall

1993.

ORDER 

New Delhi, the 20th January, 1993 

S.O. 265.—M/s. Imagene Creations, Tirupur-638607 were 
granted an import licence No. P/CG/2101164 dated 9-11-92 
for Rs. 8,08,527- (Rupees Eight Lakhs eight thousand five 
Hundred and twenty seven) import of C4 as per list enclosed 
under EPCG Scheme. 

The firm has applied for issue of Duplicate copies of Cus¬ 
toms and Exchange Control purpose of the above mentioned 
licence on the ground that the original Customs and Exchange 
crmtrol copies of the licence has been lost or misplaced. It 
has further been stated that the Customs and Exchange control 
copies of the licence were not registered with any Customs 
Authority and as such the value of Customs purpose copy 
has not been utilised at all. 

2. In support of their contention, the licensee has filed an 
affidavit on stamped paper duly sworn in before a Notary 
Public Tirupur. I am accordingly satisfied that the original 
Customs and Exchange control purpose copies of Import 
licence No. P/CG/2101I64 dt. 9-11-92 has been lost Sr 
misplaced by the firm. In exercise of the powers conferred 
under sub-clause 9(cc) of the Import (Control) Order, 1955 
dt. 7-12-1955 as amended, the said original Customs and 
Exchange Control purpose copy No. P/CG/2101164 dated 
9-11-92 issued to M/s. Imagene Creations, Tirupur is here¬ 
by cancelled. 

3. The duplicate Customs and Exchange Control purpose 
copies of ths said licence is being issued to the party sepa¬ 
rately. 

[F. No. 18/AM-93/948 /EPCG-II/l 159] 
MAYA D. KEM, Dy, Chief Controller of Imp. & Exp.

ORDER 

New Delhi, the 22nd January, 1993 

SO 266.—M/s. University of Madras, Madras-600005, 
were granted CCP No. P/J/3109396 dt. 5-10-92 for 
Rs. 56,07,704/- (Rupees Fifty six lakhs seven thousand seven 
hundred and four only) for import of Equipments us per 
list attached under GCA. 

The firm has applied for issue of Duplicate copy of the 
above mentioned Customs clearance permit on the ground 
that the original Customs clearance permit has been lost 
or misplaced. It has further been stated that the Customs 
clearance permit was not registered with any Customs Autho¬ 
rity and as such the value of Customs clearance permit has 
not been utilised at all. 

2. In support of their contention, the Firm has filed an 
affidavit on stamped paper duly sworn in before a Notary 
Public, Madras. I am accordingly satisfied that the Cus¬ 
toms clearance permit No, P/J/3109396 dt. 5-10-92 ha# 
been lost or misplnced by the firm. Ta exercise of the powers 
conferred under sub-clause 9(cc) of the Import (Conlrol) 
Order, 1955 dt. 7-12-1955 as amended the said original 
Customs clearance permit No. P/J/3109396 dt. 5-10-92 
l*sued to M/s. University of Madras, is. hereby cancelled. 

3. A duplicate Customs clearance permit is being issued to 
the party separately.

5.0. 273,—Whereas by Ihe notification of the Government 
of India in the Ministry of Coal number S.O. 2566, dated 
[lie 1st October, 1491, published in the Gazette of India, 
dated the 12th October, 1991, in Part-IT, Section 3, Sub-sec¬ 
tion (ii) at pages 3895 t* 3908, issued under sub-section (1) 
of section 7 of the Coal Bearing Areas (Acquisition and 
Development) Act, 1957 (20 of 1957) (hereinafter referred 
to as the said Act), the Central Government gave notice of 
its intention to acquire the lands described in the Schedule 
appended to that notification; 

And whereas it has beea brought to the notice of the 
Central Government that certain cnors of printing nature 
have occurred in the publication of the said notification 
in ihe Official Gazelle; 

Now, therefore, in exerciso of tho powers conferred by 
sub-section (1) of section 7 of the said Act, the Central Gov- 
vernment hereby amends the Schedule appended to the said 
notification as follows ;— 

at page 3902, in Note 2 of the notification, in expla¬ 
nation to section 6(1) of tho aforesaid -Act, for 
'‘sector" read ‘‘section"; 

iagc 3903, in the boundary description, in lino L-B, 
for ‘‘NB“ read ‘‘B’’; 

tage 3904, in the boundary description, in line c-h-i, 
for "72" read ‘‘52"; 

?uge 39(16, in the boundary description, in line I-H, 
for line heading “I-H" read "I-h” and for "H” reud 
"h"; 

in line H-G-T-Ti, for line heading “H-G-F-E” read 
"h-g-f-e” and for “E” read “e”, 
iu line E-D-C-B-A Kl, for line heading "E-D-C-B- 
A-Ki" read “e-d-e-b-a-ld” and “K.J” read ‘‘k-l, 
in line Kl-JI-ll-P, for line heading "KUMl-P" read 
"kl-jl-il-p"; 

at page 3907, in the boundary description, in line VI- 
N-f.-M for line heading "Yl-K-L-M” read ‘‘Yl-k- 
l-m” and for "point ‘M‘ ” read "point ‘m‘ 
in line R-Q for line heading "R-Q” read "r-q”, 

at page 3908, in line V-P-p- for lino heading "V-P-Q” 
read “v-p-q” aud for "Q" read "q",

MINISTRY OF URBAN DEVELOPMENT 
(Delhi Division) 

New Delhi, the 12th January, 1993 

S.O. 279.—Whereas certain modifications, which the Cen¬ 
tral Government proposes to make in the Master Plan for 
Delhi/Zonal Development Plan regarding the areas men¬ 
tioned hereunder were published vide Notice No, F. 16(10) 
86-MP dated 14-7-92 in accordance with the provisions of 
Section 44 of the Delhi Development Act, 1957 (61 of 1957) 
inviting objections /suggestions as required by sub-section (3). 
of Section 11-A of the said Act, within thirty days from the 
dale of the said Notice; 

And whereas no ijbjections and suggestions have been 
received with regard to the said proposed modification; 

And whereas the Central Government have decided to 
modify the Master Plan for Delhi /Zonal Development Plan: 

Now, therefore, in exercise of the powers conferred by 
sub-section (21 of Section 11-A of the said Act. the Central 
Government hereby makes the following modification in the 
said Master Plan for Delhi with effect from the date of 
publication of this Notification in the Gazette of India. 

MODIFICATION 

“The land use of an area measuring about 1.01 beet. 
(2.5 acres) falling in zone D-4 and bounded by 
Mabadeva Road in the North All India Radio 
Building in the East, and 60’ wide road along AIR 
and the West is proposed to be change from ‘resi¬ 
dential’ to “Govt. Offices’*.

sub-section (1) and sub-section (2A) of Section 10 of the

SCHEDULE

Employers in relation to the Management ol Madhaipur

AND

APPEARANCES: 



423 


VTTTtT TT •'.tJI'-ia : 13, IBSI/BPr 24, 1914 


[*rpT II—ij^r 3 (ii)] 

For the Employers—Sri P. Banerjee, Advocate. 


For the Workmen—Sri Bijoy Kumar Joint Secretary of 
the Union. 

INDUSTRY : Coal. STATE : West Bengal. 

Dated, the 291h December, 1992 
AWARD 

The Gove rnm ent of India in the Ministry of Labour in 
exercise of the powers conferred on them by clause (d) of 
sub-section (1J and sub-section (2A) of Section 10 ot the 
Industrial Disputes Act, 1947, has referred the following 
Oisputc to this Tribunal for adjudication vide Ministry's Order 
No, L-22012(210)/89-lR(C.li) dated 20-12-89/5-1-90. 

SCHEDULE 

“Whether the action of the Management of Madhaipur 
Colliery ot M/s. E. C. Ltd., in denying upgradation 
of S/Sri Lakhr Manta Muhherjee, Nirnai Ch. karma- 
kar, Ganesh Ed. Shaw, Badal Ch. Ghosh and Tapan 
Kr. Das as Cap Lamp Issue Clerks and payment 
of Grade II wages from 15-3-82, is justified ? If not, 
to what relief are the workmen concerned entitled 7” 

2. The case of the union in brief is that S/SUri Lakhi Manta 
Mukherjoe, Nimai Ch. karmarkar, Ganesh Pd. Shaw, Badal 
Ch. Ghosh and Tapan kr. Das were Cap Lamp Fitters of 
Madhaipur Colliery under Eastern Coalfields Ltd. They per¬ 
formed duty to the full satisfaction of the management. Being 
satisfied with the performance of their work the management 
promoted/upgraded the abovenamed workers from the post 
of Cap Lamp Litter (Gr. 11) w.c.f. 15-3-83. After such 
promotion/upgradation they began to work as Cap Lamp 
Issue Clerks. But the management never paid them the 
wages of the higher grade in qpite of request by the work¬ 
men though they had been rendering services in the post 
of higher grade. So the workman raised a dispute through 
union. But the attempts of conciliation failed. The matter 
was referred to the Ministry of l abour, Government of India 
and ultimately the Ministry of Labour has sent this dispute 
to this Tribunal for adjudication. 

3. The management has filed written objection contending 
inter alia that the representing unton has no locus-staudi to 
proceed with the case. The concerned workmen arc not 
the members of the union. It is true that all the five con¬ 
cerned workmen were Cap Lamp Fitters of Madhaipur Col¬ 
liery. The designation of the workmen was Cap Lamp Litter. 
The management issued letter of authorisation in favour of 
the said workmen to work as Cup Lamp Issue Clerk on 
15-3-82 and such authorisation was given for deploying them 
as Cap Lamp Issue Clerk on purely temporary basis (para 
12 of the written statement). The workmen were never given 
any promotion and they were never upgraded to the higher 
grade as staled by the union. So the workmen are not entitled 
to get any relief in the instant case. 

4. There was a settlement out of Court between S/Sri Lakhi 
Manta Mukherjee, Ganesh Pd. Shaw and Badal Cji. Ghosh in 
one side and the management on the other side and Ihey filed 
a joint memorandum of settlement in this Court on 6-5-91. 
On the basis of the terms of the settlement an award was 
passed in favour of those three workmen on 6-5-91. During 
the pendency of this case Nimai Ch. Karmakar died and 
his wife filed a petition to proceed with the case on behalf 
of her deceased husband. But ultimately no step was taken 
on behalf of this deceased workman and Tapan Kr, Das. 
So a no dispute award was passed on 6-5-91 with respect to 
those two workmen. 

Subsequently the case of the deceased workman Nimai 
Ch Karmahnr, represented bv his wifi* Naravani Karmakar 
and Taoan Kr. Das was restored to file by an order dated 
21-4.92. So v" are now to decide the dispute with respect 
to the claim of Nimai Ch. Karmakar (since deceased) and 
Tapan Kr. Das. 

5. Tn this ce-e r n oral or dorumcntnrv evidence has been 
adduced by either of the parties. Nimai Ch. Karmakar 
died during the pendency of this rase. Tannn Kr. Das has 
been promoted to the post of Mining Sirdar. Sri Biiov 
Kumar the learned Advocate for the workmen with his usual 
fairness submits that the union does not press the claim of 


upgradation any more as one of the workman is dead and 
the other workman has been promoted to a post of higher 
grade. He submits that the union now only claims the 
difference of pay for the period for which those two work¬ 
men worked as Lamp Issue Clerk in Gr. II. 

6 . At the very outset Sri P. Banerjee the learned Advoeato 
for the management has urged before me that the union has 
no locus-standi to represent the workmen as the workmen are 
not members of the said union. He has urged before me 
that the management has taken a specific plea on that point 
in para 4 of the written statement. Further he has urged 
before me that the union has not produced any resolution 
showing the competency of the union and there is no docu¬ 
ment to show that the workmen weie members of the 
representing union. 

It is true that the union has not produced any oral or 
documentary evidence on this point. Sri Bijoy Kumar the 
learned Advocate for the union has urged before me that no 
procedure has been laid down in the Industrial Disputes Act 
tegarding the raising of the dispute by any particular union. 
Moreover, he has urged before me that considering the prin¬ 
ciples of equity, natural justice and good conscience the 
Tribunal must hold that the union has the locus-standi to 
represent the workmen. Considering all the facts and cir¬ 
cumstances of the present case and the principles of natural 
justice, equity and good conscience I find that the union hai 
the locus-standi to represent the workmen. 

7. Now comes the question whether the workmen art. 
entitled to get any difference of pay as claimed by them. 
In para 12 of the written statement the management has 
clearly stated that letter of authorisation was issued in favour 
of the workmen on 15-3-82 to work as Cap Lamp Issue Clerk 
and they were deployed to weak ,i . i i >. .,'n.it,' on purely 
temporary basis, so they are not entitled to get any difference 
of pay. Sri Banerjee the learned Advocate for the manage¬ 
ment has further urged before me that there is nothing on 
record to show from which date to which date they actually 
worked as Cap Lamp Tssuc Clerk. As such they arc not 
entitled to get any relief. It is admitted that the workmen 
had worked as Cap Lamp Issue Clerks fot a certain period 
in Gr. II when thev were holding the post of Cap Lamp 
Fitter’s in Gr. III. Thus I find that as the authority deployed 
them to work in a post of higher category, the management 
is bound to pay the difference of wages. 

8 . In the result I find that the management was justified 
in denying upgradation of Nintai Ch. Karmakar (since deceas¬ 
ed) and Tapan Kr. Das, But the management was not jus¬ 
tified in denying the payment of Gr. II w r ages from the daft 
Nimni Ch. Karmakar (since deceased) and Tapan Kr. Das 
worked as Cap I.anip Issue Clerk. Consequently I find that 
Nimai Ch. Karmakar (since deceased) and Tapan Kr. Das 
are entitled to get the difference of wages of the post of 
Can Lamp Fitters (Gr. Ill) and the post of Can Lamp Issue 
Clerk (Gr. IT) for the period (hey had actually worked in 
the post of Cap Lamp Issue Clerk in Gr. TT. 

The management is directed to pay such difference of wages 
within three months from the date of publication of the 
award, failing w'hich the amount wfll carry 15 per cent inte¬ 
rest from the date of publication of the award till the date 
of payment. 

This is my award. 

N. K. SAHA. Presiding Officer

’be concerned workman was emnloved in continuous, service 
for mm-e than one year under his employer. But his em- 
plo- ?r has not admittedly complied with the condition prece¬ 
dent to retrenchment as envisaged under Sec. 25-F of the 
I.D Act. This section envisages fa) that the concerned 
workman ’should be given one month’s notice indicating the 
meson for retrenchment and the period of notice expires or 
the workman hrs been paid in lien of such notice wages for 
the period of notice; (b) that the workman has been paid, 
at the time of retrenchment, compensation which shall be 
the -mohiment to 15 d-’V.-d a'.era pc ’or every completed vear 
of continuous efrvice or anv part thereof in excess of six 
mo uhs: and fc) notice in the prescribed manner is fient on 
the '"’r-ronrjpm Government or such authority as may be 
specified by the 'appropriate Government. 

Admittedly, the management has not complied with any 
of the e provisions. 

9. But the management has sought to Justify its action by 
taking the stance that the initial appointment and his 
continuance in service by the Asstt. Manager CD) Food 



THE GAZETTE OF INDIA : FEBRUARY 1.3, 1993/MAGHA 24, 1914 


[Part II—Sec. 3(ii)] 
MATATV 1993 


Storage Depot, Jozidih, as casual workman was illegal and 
so it shall be deemed that he was never engaged at all. In 
support of this contention the case of Bijoy Kumar Bliarti 

6 others Vs. State of Bihar nrd others reported in 1933 
Lab. I.C. 1884 (Patna) has been cited. I am constrained to 
state that no such proposition of law' us mentioned by ihe 
management is enshrined in this decision. The Court has 
held that any Workman appointed illegally or irregularly hi", 
no right to claim or continue in the post. Their Lotdsh'ps 
.have not decided that the provisions of Sec. 2J-F of the 
I.D. Act arc not applicable to such workman, Admittedly, 
the concerned workman was engaged hy the F.C.l, and lie 
worked for more than 240 days in 1986 and 1987. That 
being so, the management is hound to comply with the 
provision of Sec. 25-F of the I.D. Act before terminating his 
service by way of retrenchment. Hence, I consider that the 
action of the management in terminating the service of the 
concerned workman without complying with the provisions 
of Sec. 25-F of the I.D. Act is illegal and unjustified. 

10. The management has taken the stance in its written 
statement that the concerned workman secured the employ¬ 
ment with the collusion of some interested person who is 
not authorised to appoint or allow continuance of job beyond 

7 days under emergency condition. It appeals from the 
evidence that Rajendra Sinha was the Depot Inchargc of 
Josidih at the time when the concerned workman was 
engaged (F.xts. W-4 M-6). There is not a whit of evidence 
on record to indicate that the concerned workman secured 

mployment in collusion with Sri Sinha. MW-1 A. K. Roy 
lias stated that on enquiry he found that the concerned 
workman used to come once in a while at the Depot and 
used to sewe ‘amc glasses of water, and that he got infor¬ 
mation arj indication that he was working in the house of 
the Asstt, Manager. The concerned workman has denied 
these allegtions. Shri Roy has not provided Ihe source of 
information or indication that the concern'd worknnn was 
working in the home of the Asstt. Manager. In the com¬ 
ments provided to the Asst!. Labour Commissioner (C) the 
management .has not taken this stance (Ext. W-l). The 
documents produced by the management do not also support 
the stance of the management. That being so. I consider 
that the statement of Sri Roy with regard to the work 
done by the concerned workman at the Depot and his work¬ 
ing in the house of the Asstt. Manager i>. embelishment of 
fact in order to lend succour to the case of the management. 

11. The evidence of the concerned workman discloses that 
he used to get his wages from the District office, Bhagalpur. 
His rejoinder to the management’s written staCment discloses 
that hit appointment was known to the District Manager, 
Bhagalpur as the wages were being paid by the District 
Manager, Bhagalpur. This statement of f >Ct has remained 
unassailcd. The District MsnagCr. Bhagalpur, 4 nppears. 
disapproval of the engagement of the concrncd workman 
as carnal workman soroeFme in th; Inst prut of October. 
1987 (Ext. W-4—M-6). 

12. The concerned workman has claimed regidaris'Uion in 
service as Class IV employee. Tn .hits statement, he has 
ptated that his duty roster was at par with regul.-r work¬ 
man. He has further .stated that during 1C88-8 1 ’ 1 many of 
the casual workmen were regularised in service. His written 
statement discloses that they were regularised in service ns 
Whtchmen. But there is no evidence on record to indicate 
that any post of Watchman has remained vacant cither at 
Josidih or elsewhere. Hence, his claim for reguktrisatiou 
as Watchman is not sustainable. But the management should 
try to regularise him in service if and when any vacancy 
in Class IV post arises. 

13. Accordingly, the following award !•: rendered- (he 
action of the management in terminating the service of the 
concerned workman with effect from 21-1-88 is not justified. 
The management E directed to rr-instatc him in service a*i 
casual workman with efiric' from the date of his termination 
of service and to p 1 ” him full back wages within one month 
f-r>m the date of cublicarioa of the award The managem-nt 
should consider his claim for regularization in service ns 
Class TV employee as and when vrican.v in such post arises. 
The concerned worl man (Pavan Kumar Pam) is directed to 
report for d::*y within 'he period prescribed. 

In the circnmstar,-es of (he case, T award no cost.

BEFORE ARYIND KUMAR, PRESIDING OFFICER,

AWARD 


Central Government vide Gazette Notification No L 72G12/ 
I10/88D.UI (A) dated 18th January 1989 issued U/S 10(1) 
td ) of the I. D. Act 1947 referred the following depute to 
this liihuual for adjudication : 

“Whether the action of the State Bank of India in denying 
the benefits of officiating in cash department to 
Shri Amur Chund, Cleik-cum-cashier in Stctor-7 
brunch Chandigarh of State Bank of India is legal 
and testified ? If not, to what relief the concerned 
week man is entitled to and from what date '!” 

2. In the statement of claim it hH S been alleged that the 
petitioner |oincd the services of the Bank ns clerk-cum-casbicr 
at Kuza Brunch in the year 1980 and ultimately tr uisferred to 
Sector-7 branch Chandigarh on 31-10-1984. It is further 
pleaded that his services used to be utilised both m tire accounts 
wing as well as in cash wing. It is further pleaded thru it was 
settled that the management shall ask for the option of the 
employee lor me purpose of oiiieiuting eidier ; a caih w.ng 
cr accounts w.ng. It is pleaded that the branch manager 
Sector-7 branch did not call for such options. It is pleaded 
that there was dispute in regard to the ofHciatirg and acci rd- 
ingly the petitioner submitted his option to officiate in cash wing 
011 23-7-1986. However the petitioner was not allowed to 
officiate in the cash wing. The petitioner represented to the 
Deputy General Manager who decided 10 allow hini l-j officiate 
;.s per his option dated 18-10-1986. But the petitioner was not 
allowed to otfy.it te in the cash wing by the branch manager. It 
is further pleaded that the Union raised ihe industrial dispute 
on 15-9-1987 in which the management agreed in conciliation 
to allow him to work in cash wing provided he submits another 
option. The petitioner submitted fresh option on 15-9-1937. 
The petitioner was allowed to officiate in cash wing however 
for a day only on 16-9-1987 and again denied the officiating in 
the said w-ng. It is further pleaded that in denying the offi¬ 
ciating to the petitioner as per his option in the cash wing is 
illegal and unjustified and mala fide. Jt is therefore, prayed that 
the management be directed to allow the petitioner to officiate 
in the cash wing us per his option dated 23-7,1986 and the 
petitioner be paid officiating allowance for whole of the 
period. 

3. Claim of the petitioner was contested. The respdt, has 
taken the stand that the petitioner had been working and 
officiaing in accounts wing since his joining in the branch, 
Respdt. admitted that the petitioner submitted his option on 
23-7-1986 to work and not to officiate in the cash w'ing as 
per circular under reference. It is further pleaded that the 

etitioner has not right to opt for officiating in cash wing 
ecause he was already officiating in the accounts wing since 
his joining. Therefore, impliedly ho is deemed to have cpied 
for officiating in the accounts wing. It is, therefore submit¬ 
ted that there is no distinction of pecuniary benefits for offi¬ 
ciating m either of the deptts. It is further pleaded that 
the officiating powers can not be claimed as a matter of right 
as per Bank’s norms laid down in the paragraph No. 446. 
Therefore, it is clear that suitability of the concerned staff 
has to he seen while entrusting him the officiating powers. 
Since the petitioner was already working and officiating in the 
accounts wing, therefore, he had some experience In that wing 
and it was thus in the interest of the hank to utilise his 
services in the accounts wing because he lacks experience in 
the cash department. Other contentions in the claim state¬ 
ment were denied. 

4. Replication was also filed reasserting the contents made 
fax the claim statement. 

5. The petitioner in support of his case examined himself 
as WW-1 and tendered his affidavit Ex. W-l in evidence. 
Respdt. management produced Shri S. K. Sharma who pro¬ 
duced himself as MW-1 and tendered his affidavit Ex. M-l 
in evidence. Workman also got proved circular No, 298/80 
as WW-2 and closed their caaC. 

6 . I have heard both the parlies and gone through the 
evidence and record on the file. The representative appearing 
on behalf of the workman has argued that the petitioner bad 
opted to work in the cash wing in pursuance of the decision 
taken in the Award staff Circle joint consultative committee 
where in it has been decided that the option be asked from 
all Ihe cnshicr-cum-clcrks as to where they would like to 
officiate, in accounts or cash department and the, officiate 
powers could he entrusted accordingly, however inspite of the 
option the petitioner was not allowed to work in cash wing. 


429 


There is no force in the contention raised by the representa¬ 
tive of the workman. The petitioner Amar Chaud in his own 
cross-examination has admitted that the officiating assignment 
is given against leave or temporary vacancy at the discretion 
of the hank on the basis of suitability and seniority. Ilo has 
also admitted that lie had worked in Sector-7 branch since 
1984 and during the said period he had worked in the accounts 
wiDg. In these circumstances the petitioner is more suitable 
to woik in tiie accounts wing ihan in cash wing. However 
as per bank’s law in para No. 446 of the Reference Book on 
staff matters it is provided that oificiaing/relief powers are not 
be granted to the members of ihe stall as a matter of routine 
without examining the necessity Ihercof vis-a-vis the work 
situation and the seniority/suitnbiii'y of the concerned member 
of the staff, for the job. ft further contains that the relief 
powers can not be demanded as a matter of right. Officiating 
powers, therefore, should be given onlv when it is deemed 
necessary. Thus in view of the situation the petitioner can not 
claim officiating in the cash wing as a matter of right and he 
was allowed to work in the accounts wing since lie had suffi¬ 
cient experience and the decision taken in the joint Award staff 
committee are for the clean administration and not for mis- 
ulilising the provisions contains therein. Admitted position is 
that there is no difference of pecuniary benefits for officiating 
either in cash wing or in the accounts wing. 

In view of the discussion made in the earlier paras there 
is no merit in the claim of the petitioner ard the same is 
dismissed. Reference is returned accordingly,, 

Chandigarh. 

ARVTND KUMAR. Presiding Officer

purport of his case th e i petitioner examined himself

■ -c : ii cr( ^- s -cxaminaiion he admitted that the petitioner was

tIie P;) rties have filed written arguments and I have 
neaid them orally as well. It has been stressed that the ter¬ 
mination of the services of tho petitioner is illegal and the 
management has violated the provisions of Section 25G & H 
ot tie l.D Act 1947 as they have recruited largo number of 
per sons attcr the termination of services of the petitioner. 

here is no force in this contention. Provisions of Para 20 8 
of the Bipartite Settlement dated 19th October, 1965 stipu- 
ales that the bank is within its competence to appoint tem¬ 
porary workmen against permanent vacancies for three months 
during which period the bank shall make arrangement for 
till mg up the vacancies permanently. In this context the ap¬ 
pointment of, the petitioner was made, Ex. M2 is tho appoint- 


1993/MAGHA 24, 1914 [Part II— Sec. 3(ii)] 


mem letter which clearly, stipulates that ihe appointment 
ot ihe petitioner is only fol' the fixed period up lo 3rd January, 
1979 and alter the expiry of that period by eiiiux of time the 
services of the petitioner automatically stands icrmmaled. 
Certainly mere is no dispute to the tact that the extension 
was given to the petitioner and he had worked only upto 22nd 
February, 19/9 and thus has put in only 78 days ot total 
service. He was appointed a s temporaiy hand and according 
to the terms and conditions of his appointment letter itself 
contained the order ot termination of his setviccs as will for 
which the petitioner himself gave his acceptance and joined 
the duty and thus the respondent management has not contra¬ 
vened any provisions of Bipartite Settlemeint/Desni Award. 

7. Another plea raised by the petitioner is witli regard to 
(he violation ot Section 25-H of the J,D. Act and contended 
that targe number of person^ has been employed by Ihe Res¬ 
pondent bank alter the termination of services of the petitio¬ 
ner. There is also no force in this contention. To piove this 
violation the petitioner is heavily burdened to adduce sufficient 
evidence. In his pleadings or in his evidence het has not stated 
that to whom the respondent management has given employ¬ 
ment and when. There is also no evidence that the post still 
existed even alter the termination of services of the petitioner. 
No doubt Mr, R. D. Duggal MWI appearing on behall of the 
management has stated that in the event of exigencies they 
have appointed sub staff after the completion of temporary 
tenure of tho workman but the workman has failed to clari¬ 
fy when those vacancies had existed. In ibis circumstances 
it can not bo said that the post against Which the petitioner 
was working continued after the termination of the services 
of the petitioner. The ratio of the judgement reported in 
1991(3) Services Cases today page 127 Slate of Punjab Vs. 
Ram Murti is followed. Not only this the petitioner in his own 
cam in the statement of claim has admitted that in the year 
1987 the respondent management had issued circular for 
affording opportunity for employment to all the ex-tejuporary 
employees and his request was denied. Thus in his own case 
us apparent above the management has complied with the 
provision;, of Section 25-H of the l.D. Act 1947. 

In view of (he discussion made in earlier paras, the action 
ot the management in terminating the services of tho petitioner 
is justified and the workman is not entitled to any relief. 
However, I must express my concern for the poor workman 
since he had already worked with the respondent management 
and has ait experience to tho post. I therefoie, recommend to 
the respondent management to give opportunity to the peti¬ 
tioner for the post subj'eet to the availability as an act of 
benevelcnce or goodwill gesture on their part. The award is 
returned accordingly,

AWARD 

The Government of India in the Ministry of labour, in 
exercise of its power conferred by clause (d) of sub-section 
(J) and sub-scction (2A) of Section 10 of the Industrial 
Disputes Act, 1947 (14 ol 1947), have referred the following 
dispute for adjudication vide their Order No. L-27012/2/90- 
JR (Misc.), dated 5-7-1990 :— 

“Whether the action of the management of Shri Hukum 
Singh Kama! contractor, Silijoda Kulimati Manga¬ 
nese' Mines of M s. Mangilal Rungta (P) Ltd. At/P. 
O. Sdjoda, Via Joda, Distt. Keonjhar in terminal- 
ing I he services of Shri Benuchar Pradhan, Muir-, hi 
w.e.t. 3-9 L9H8 is justified ? If not, to what relief 
Is iC.c workman entitled ?’’ 

2. Briefly stated the case of the workman is that he was 
a Muuslii under Hukum Singh Kamal, a contractor ol Siljoda- 
Kalimatj Manganese Mines of M/s, Mangilal Rungta (P) 
Ltd. since 5-9-1978 and without any rifynie and reason his 
employer terminated him from services on 3-9-1988. Smell 
action of his master being illegal he has sought for icinstale- 
ment with full back wages mid other consequential benefits. 

3. Both thr contractor as well as the principal employer 
have filed heparate written statements. In nutshell the case 
of the contractor, the Immediate employer, is that though 
the workman was under his employment Since 1978 but 
during tenure of his service he very often remained absent 
unauthorisciHy and finally be was unheard of since 23-2-88 
till 3-9-1988. This necessitated the cmplover to iVue him 
a notice under certille*ate of posting at hi:< village address, 
Dcspilc of such notice when he did not respond, his name 
war stttick off from the roils, Ii is further alleged that the 



THE GAZETTE OF INDIA : FEBRUARY 13, 1993/MACHA 24, 1914 [Part II— Sec. 3(ii)J 


workman while in service w.is involved wilh various offences. 
He committed rape on two minor girls and enticed a mar¬ 
ried woman. So, appiehending assault at the hands of the 
pubhe lor hi> nnloiions activities he left the job and w.it, 
unhenid of foi a conridcruble period. In such a. situation 
there wio no other alternative than to adopt such eor.ise 
by deleting his name horn employ merit. 

It is needless to refer to the written statement of the 
principal employei in view of the term of reference (o as- 
oeitain if me. action of the immediate employer, namely, 
the contractor in to initialing the workman from his services 
is legal and justified. 

4. In course of hearing both parties, namely, the work¬ 
man and the contractor led evidence in support of their 
respective case. J he management examined two witnesses 
and proved cei taut documents. Of the two witnesses, wit¬ 
ness No, 1 vjscaks tlutt a notice was 'sent lo the workman 
hy registered post asking him to join his service. On the 
other h ind, the employer on being examined as witness 
No. 2 has stated that He sent such a notice undet certificate 
of posting. He has proved (he copy of the notice, Ext. 1 
and the postal receipt, Ext. 2. It is borne out from the 
said notice that tile woihmun was intimated that unless he 
joined his duty within fifteen days bis name would be 
struck oil from the register. It transpires from the 
postal seal pul on the receipt Ext. 2 that though the let tor 
way sent to the workman but the date appearing thereon 
has been intcipointed. Therefore, it is doubtful whethet 
any notice was at all sent under certificate of posting ns 
deposed to hy M. VV. 2. If at all the employer sincerely 
desired to inform the workman that if he did not join his 
duty hiv; name would be struck off from the Roll, tnen m 
that case he would have sent such notice by registered 
Post. 

The employer (M.W. 2) lias proved tho attendance regis¬ 
ter, Ext. 3 to show t'he continued absence ol’ the workman 
from duty. The genuineness of the said register is doubt 
as because it dries not bear eitther initial, or signature of the 
person tit charge of marking the attendance of the employed. 
This apart, when the fact of some employees leaving their 
Service finds mention in ((he remarks column of the atten¬ 
dance register, Ext. 3 it wus however not so stated in the 
case of the workman if at all he left uhe service tind remain¬ 
ed unheard of for a pretty long lime. 

It, is the consistent case of the employer that due: to long 
absence of tho workman his name was struck off from the 
Rolls but a peiusal of the attendance register for the month 
of Soptemiber would indicate otherwise. Instead of striking 
otf his name the employer terminated his service as men¬ 
tioned in the remarks column. In view of such udtnisssion, 
f am, inclined to bdljeve the case of the workman that his 
services were terminated on 3-9-1988. 

5. The workman has given his own statement and proved 
two documents;, Exes. A & B. As deposed lo by him, after 
his services were terminated he sent a letter, Ext. A under 
the registcicd cover. Ext B intimatling his employer to lake 
him back to employment and to pay him wages. That 
letter returned unserved on being refused by the addressee. 
It does not stand to reason as to why the employer refused 
to receive the registered letter, Ext. B if at all he sincerely 
dcsiied (hat the workman should continue in his service. On 
the contrary from ihe pleadings coupled with (he evidence 
and circumstances, a.si discussed above, it would reveal that 
the employer being annoyed wilh the alleged notorious uiti- 
vjties of ihe workman which had no nevus with Ihe duty 
wanted to remove him from service and ultimately he did so 
by terminating hi K services on 3-9-1988, 

6 . In view of my discussions made above. I hold that 
termination of service of the workman is illegal and un¬ 
justified and so, he be reinstated in service and paid all back 
wages. Payment be made by his employer, namely, Shri 
IHukiun Singh Kfimal within n period of three months from 
die date of 'publication of Ihis Awmd. 

7. The leforcitrc is answered accordingly.

AWARD 

The Central Government by their Order No. L-31012/7/90- 
IR (Misc.) dated -7-1990 have referred the following indus¬ 
trial dispute to this Tribunal for adjudication under Section 
10(l)(d) of the Industrial Disputes Act, 1947. 

‘Whether the action of tho management of M/s. Seascau 
Services Pvt. Ltd., Bombay, in transferring Shri Dilip 
R, Naik, Tally Clerk from Bombay Office to Tutrcqrin 
office w.e.f. 21-11-19RS and subsequently teiminating 
his services w.e.f. 26-11-1988, is justified 7 If not, 
to what relief is the workman entitled 7” 

2. The ense of the workman Shri D, R, Naik as disclosed 
from the Statement of Claim (Ex. W-2) filed on his behalf hy 
the Secretary of the B.P.T. Employees’ UnioD in short is 
thus :— 

The said workman is a permanent employee of M/s. 
Seascan Services Pvt. Ltd., Bombay, working as a 
Clerk w.p.f. 17-6-1985. He took active part in 
organising the workman under the banner of the

REASONS 

7. Issues Nos. 1 & 2. 

The workman Shri D. R. Naik filed his affidavit (Ex, W/5) 
in support of his case, and lie was cross-txumined on behalf 
of the company. Shri AT. . Wagle, the Manager of the 
said company, filed his affidavit (Ex. M/8) in support of their 
ease, and he was cross-examined on behalf of the workman. 
According to the management of the said company, the 
Central Government is not thp appropriate Government in 
respect of that company, but that the Government of Maha¬ 
rashtra is the appropriate Government, and that the Cential 
Government Industrial Tribunal has no jurisdiction to enter¬ 
tain and dealt- the present relerence. However, the present 
iofeicnee has been made by the Central Government. T-urthr, 
the conciliation proceedings were held by the Asst. Labour 
Commissionr (Cential). As such, it will be presumed that 
tile Central Government is the appiopriale 1 Government in 
defect of the company in question, unless some material 
is placed on jceord to show that the Government of Muharush- 
i<a is the appropriate Government in icspecl of that company. 
No such material has been placed on iccoid, except the baie 
statement that the said company ri registered under the 
Bombay Shops and Establishment Act, 1948. According to the 
said company the Central Government Industrial Tribunal, 
No. 1. Bombay has he'll in sonic other reference that the 
Government of MahnraTura is the mpivpiiate Government as 
regards the company question. However, the judgment of that 
court No. 1 is not binding upon this cumt. Therefore, for the 
reasons mentioned above. T hud tb.it the Cential Government 
I* appropi Sale Government in re-nect of the Company in 
lo'csiion and as such, the present Central Government Indust¬ 
rial Tribunal has jurisdiction to entertain and decide the 
present reference. 

ISSUES NOS, 1 and ? are therefore found in ihe affirmative. 

8. ISSUE NOS. 3 and 4 

According to the workman, the su'd company, after issuing 
the order of titmxfc'r to Tuticorin, has terminated his services. 
However, the vM company has made n categorical statement 
in their Written Sia’cmcnt. and the witness for the said 
company also made clear a statement in his affidavit that 
the sat,i company has not in fact terminated ihe services of 
the said workman, but that tl-e company is still prepared to 
accent him in services ; ,t Tuticorin in east- he joins for 
duty there. It is true that the name of the said workman 
has been removed from Ihe muster u,1l of Hie company at 
Bond'''" However, a- the older was is ale,I by the compam 
traie f erring him from Bombay to Tuticorin. the name of 
that workman was removed fjom the muster roll of Bombay

Tally Cl‘n; from Bombay Office to Tutici n office 
w.e.f. 2H1-19B8 and subsequently terminating bis

The management of Tile said comptinv is hereby directed

only. Tver; then according tq the said company, 1 he said 

wotkntan is still in their services. I. therefore, find that the

the said workman, but has only issued an order transferring

As such. Issue No. 4 does not survive. 

9. ISSUE NO. 5 

Ex. M JO is a letter of appointment dated 7-7-1987 of 
the said company appointing^ the said workman Shri D. R. 
N:uk in tile sciviccs of the company. This letter clearly 
stated that the company has a right to transfer him at its 
other offices us and when the necessity arises. '1 he company 
by their letter dated 21-11-1988 (Ex. M/12) transferred the 
said workman Irorn Bombay to Tuticorin. By this letter the 
workman was informed that his secovid class train fare, front 
Bombay to Tuticorin will be borne by the company, und that 
his lodging boarding charges of the first 7 days’ will also 
be borne by the company and thereafter the workman would 
be inquired to make his own arrangements. Immediately 
2 days after the issue of the said letter of transfer, the 
workman by his letter dated 23-11-1988 inionned the company 
that lie would find it difficult lo get accommodation at 
Tuticorin that he is quite new to that place and that he 
may not please be trnnsfcried to Tuticorin. The company 
bv heir reply of the same dale, i.e. 23-11-1988 (Ex. M/14) 
informed (he workman that his transfer was on business 
exigencies and that he should report for duty to Tuticorin 
within a week. Then workman again by his letter dated 
23-11-1988 (E\. M/1J) requested the company not to transfer 
him from Bombay to Tuticorin, and stated that he may be 
allowed to continue in services in Bombay only, The company 
by I heir Idler dated 24-11-1988 (Ex. M. 16) mloi med the 
wmhman that his request could not be acccpled. and in case 
he would not report fur duty at the new place, it would be a 
serious misconduct on his paif. The workman again by his 
letter dated 28-11-1988 (Bx. M/18) informed the company 
that the action of the company in transferring him from 
Bombay to Tuticorin was malaflde, and further stated that 
he i s willi ig lo continue in services in Bombay only. It 
is true that ns per the terms and conditions embodied in 
the letter lor appointment, the company had every right 
to hnnsfer the said workman from Bombay to any other plage 
in Tndiu, However I find that the action of the Bombay 
ill the matter was not just and proper. The said workman 
was getting ,i meagre pay of Rs, 750 per month. It is 
true that his lodging and boarding charges of the first 
seven days of Tuticorin and his travelling expenses from 
Bombay lo Tuticorin were to be home by the company. 
However, after one week, the ’necessary arrangements lor 
lodging and boarding were to be made by the workman 
himself. The workman was transferred from Bombay to 
Tuticorin only within 1 1/2 years after he joined his 
services with the company at Bombay. By the said letter 
of transfer, that workman was transferred from Bombay 
t.t Tuticorin which is a very far away place from Bombav 
and he is quite new to that place. Further n« he himself 
was. to make his arrangements for lodging and boarding one 
week after joining there, he would find it very difficult 
to make necessary arrangements in the matter. As such 
the compa'nv should not have transferred him from 
Bombay to Tuticorin, eventhough the company was entitled 
to transfer him from Bombay to any other place in India. 

T therefore find that eventhough the action of the company 
in the matter cannot be consider as illegal, still it is 
not just and proper. The reference made: by the Central 
Government is also whether the action of the company 
in the mailer is justified, (and not whether it in Ie<>al). 
T, therefore, find that the action of the company in the matter 
is not just and proper. 

Issue No. 5 Is therefore find In the negative. 

The relief to be granted to the workman therefore 
is that the company management ffmst be directed to issue 
an order transferring him from Tuticorin to Bombay, ami 
to direct the workman to join his duty in the Bombay 
office 

II. The following Award is therefore passed :

AWARD 

The Government of India In the Ministry of Labour in 
exercise of powers conferred upon it by elause(d) of sub¬ 
section (I) and s ub-scction (2A) of Section 10 of the Indus¬ 
trial Disputes Act, 1947 (14 of 19 47) have referred the 
following dispute for adjudication vido their Order No, L- 
2601l!ll[86-D.m.B|rV.B dated 3rd Sept. ’87 : 

“Whether the action of the management of Orissa Mining 
Corporation, Bhubaneswar in transferring Sri T.K. 
Ghosh, Tractor Compressor Oporator from Banspani 
Iron Mine of Barbil Zone to Daitari Iron Mine of 
Daitnri Zonei and P.C. Chhotray, Tractor Compres¬ 
sor Operator from Dubna Mn. Mine of Barbil Zone 
to Munduguda Mine of Jaipur Zone with effect 
from 81h January, 1986 is malafide 7 If so, to 
what relief the workmen arc entitled 7" 

2 . It is needless to refer to the pleadings of the parties 
to give u finding on the question as to whether the transfer 
of two ngrieved workmen, namely, T.K. Ghosh and P. C. 
Chhotray effected by the management was malafide or not 
for the reason that a compromise has been entered into 
between Sri T. K. Ghosh and the management and in so far 
a; the other workman Sri P. C. Chhotray is concerned, he 
has prayed the Tribunal by a separate petition to drop the 
proceeding. As there is no provision in the Industrial Dis¬ 
putes Act cither to drop or dismiss a reference, I um required 
to give a finding and answer the same. 

3. The compromise effocted between Sri T.K, Ghosh and 
the management being fair and proper shall form part of 
the award. In so far as the workman Sri P.C. Chhotray is 
concerned, though the action of the management has been 
challenged in the statement of claims but no evidence has 
been led to show that with malafide intention the management 
transferred him from one place to another. This apart, when 
the workman himself does not wunt to proceed with the 
case, it appears that there exist no dispute to be adjudicated 
upon. Hence, a no dispute award is passed in so far as Sri 
P.C. Chhotray is concerned. 

Dictated and corrected by me. 

R. K. DASH, Presiding Officer

9. The coneerued workman has taken the stance in his reply 
to the chargesheet that the charge levelled against him is vaguo 
and ambiguous. 

Shii D. Mukhcrjce, authorised representative of the con¬ 
cerned workman, has submitted that the charge suffers from 
infirmity of \aguencss and ambiguity. 

It is essential that charge shall contain such particulars 
as to the time and place of alleged offence 1 and the person, 
if any, against whom, or thing, if any, in respect of which 
it is committed ns are reasonably sufficient to give the person 
proceeded against notice of the matter with which he is 
charged, When tliei nature of the case is such that the parti¬ 
culars mentioned above do not give the person proceeded 
against sufficient notice of the matter with which be is charg¬ 
ed, tho charge shall ulso contain such particulars of the matter 
in which the alleged offence was committed as will bo suffi¬ 
cient for the piupose. 

Upon perusal of the. chargesheet it appears that it has 
icvcalcd the time and place of the misconduct, but the parti¬ 
culars of the manner in which the misconduct was committed 
have not been spelt out precisely. I consider that it was boun- 
den duty of the management to spell out tho particulars of the 
manner in which the alleged misconduct was committed by 
the concerned workman. That not being done, I hold that the 
charge suffers front infirmity of vagueness and ambiguity. 

10. Shri D. Muklicrjee has further submitted that the charge- 
sheet was issued under Clause 17(l)(a) of the Model Stand¬ 
ing Orders applicable to Moonidih Washery by the Project 
Officer, Moonidih Washcry und that there is no evidence od 
record to show that the Project Officer, Moonidih Washery 
ivas the Agent or Manager of Moonidih Colliery and Washery. 
According to him, the charge-sheet was issued by an incompe¬ 
tent person. 

Shri B. Joshi has submitted that Moonidih Colliery and 
Washery was under N.C.D.C. and that the Corporation had 
the Certified Standing Orders of its own. Clause 17(l)(a) of 
the Certified Standing Orders is comparable to Clause 17(1) (a) 
uf the Model Standing Orders and powers were delegated to 
all Project Officers of the Washcry. In terms of Clause 17(2) 
of the Certified Standing Orders (Coal Mines) the power for 
an approval of punishment has referred, to in the Certified 
Standing Orders was given to Project Officers among others. 
This may be the position with regard to the establishment 
tinder N.C.D.C., but here tbc chargesheet was Issued under 
the provision of Model Standing Orders. There is ho evidence 
t-i indicate that the Project Officer of Moonidih Washery was 
the .Agent of Moonidih Colliery and Washery’. Hence, 1 find 
substance in the contention of Shri Mukherjee that the evi¬ 
dence of the management is faulted on the score that the 
charge sheet was issued W » competent person.

11. It appears that although tut cnurgesiiecl was tssuea

12 . li appeals trom the enquiry lepoil that the iniquity 
Unitei has noticed somei 'cucungs and overwritings’ in me 
absentee statement 01 tne workmen, jn his statement boiote 
me linqiiuy onlcer, the concerned workman Has stated mat 
ttic absentee statements reach the Personnel Department alter 
nutting tluough all the departments and from the Personnel 
Department tne statements reach thei Planning Oiliest and 
tbeiealtei die statements reach tne pill Section with the sig¬ 
nature of the Planning Office]. According to absentee repou, 
0 .1. Sunday, Haz.au or whatever more ts payable is noted 
in the attendance format and then alter obtaining the signature 
of the Personnel Department the statement is sent to Machine 
neclion. The Enquiry Officer has held thut while filling up 
the format of the bill it was duty of Ifie concerned workman 
to see that 'cutting & overwritings’ in the absentee statement 
must be countersigned by the Departmental Head in order to 
ascertain authenticity of checking. According to him, if there 
is no signature against such cuttings and overwritings he 
should have immediately approached to get the signature of 
the concerned Sectional Head or he should have reported the 
mailer to tne Project Officer to get the ming done. In other 
words, the Enquiry Officer has put entire buck on the con¬ 
cerned workman sparing the concerned Departmental Head 
and other employees whose duly it was to countersign the 
so-called ‘cuttings and overwritings’. In hi s reply to the charge- 
sheet the concerned workman has written to the Project 
Officer that ‘while verifying a nd rectifying the O.T, sent by 
the l ime Section, the Departmental Head used to make u lot 
of cuttings and re-writings in the sheets which has always 
been communicated to the Ex-Project Officer and to you. I 
have also objected many times for billing on the basis of 
such sheets which bear s lot of cutting but I was all the time 
used to prepare formut of bill by the Ex-Projcct Officer and 
laTer on by you’. Such is the asseveration of the concerned 
workman 111 his reply to the chargesheet and in the circum¬ 
stances I consider that it wa s the duty of the management 
to examine the concerned Project Officer to vouch for the fact 
us to whether the statement of the concerned workman as 
revealed in his reply to the chargesheet is correct or not. Any¬ 
way, from the evidence on record there is reason to doubt 
ns to whether so-called ‘cuttings and overwritings' in the 
absentee statements were done by the concerned workman. 

13. The charge against the concerned workman is for theft, 
ft and or dishonesty in connection with employers business or 
properly. In the present case thcro is no ingradient of theft 
having been committed by the concerned workman. He has 
made no personal gain by over payment to 13 different work¬ 
men. No mem-rea for commission of theft or dishonesty has 
been suggested or proved by the management, Shrl D. Mukhor- 
jee has submilled that the management has since recovered 
excess payments made to the employees from the employees 
concerned. No denial of this fact has been made by the man¬ 
agement. Deceit is an essential element of fraud. But there is 
no evidence on record to indicate that the concerned work 
man has resorted to deceit in order to strike the transactions. 

Not it remains to be considered whether he has acted 
dishonestly in the matter. Whoever does anything with the 
intention of causing wrongful gain to one person or wrongful 
loss to another person is said to do that thing dishonestly. 
Wrongful gain Is the gain by unlawful means Of property to 
which the person gaining is not legally entitled. 

Wrongful loss is loss by unlawful means of property to 
which the person losing it is legally entitled. 

A person Is said to gain wrongfully, when such person re¬ 
tains wrongfully, ns well as when such person acquires wrong¬ 
fully. A person is said to lose wrongfully when such person 
iii wrongfully keep out of any property as well as when such 
person is wrongfully deprived of property. 

The concerned workman has not gained anything by excess 
payment. He Lii s not also retained anything wrongfully. Apart 
ttom this, the intention of his causing wrongful loss to his 
employer is absolutely wanfing. This being so, I hold firmly 
that the concerned workman was not guilty of theft, fraud 
or dishonesty in connection with company’s property or busi¬ 


ness. Hence, in my view, the Enquiry Officer was not justified 
in holding the concerned workman guilty of the charge and 
subsequently the dismissal of the concerned workman from 
service by the management is not justified. He should be re¬ 
instated In service with effect from the date of dismissal from 
service with full back wages, continuity of service and other 
benefits. 

14. Accordingly, the following award is rendered the action 
of the management of Moonidih Project of M/s. B.C.C. Ltd., 
P O. Moonidih, District Dhanbad, in dismissing Jagdish Pra- 
-ad, Clerk Gr. II with effect from 20th/21st June, 1990 is not 
justified. The: management is directed to reinstate him in ser¬ 
vice with effect from the date of his dismissal from service 
and to pay him back wages from the aforesaid date with 
continuity of service and other benefits. The management is 
further directed to reinstate him in service within one month 
front die date of publication of tho award. The concerned 
workman is also directed to report for duty within the time 
stipulated. 

In the circumstances of the case, I award no cost. 

S. K. MITRA, Presiding Officer

For the Opp. Party—Shri B. Joshi, Advocate. 

STATE : Bihar. INDUSTRY : Coal. 

Dated, the 8th Jaminiy, 1993 
ORDER 

The applicant, Radhcshyom, has preferred this present 
application under Section 33-02) of the Industrial Disputes 
Act against the Opp. Party, his employer. Manager, Loya- 
h'ud Colliery of M/s, B.C.C, Ltd. laying a claim for 
Rs. 23,851.51. 

2. The case of the applicant is that lie was appointed in 
M/s. B.C.C, Ltd, in March, 19S1 and acquired permanent 
status. But he was stopped from work from 10-11-87 
wrongly. He was directed to submit fresh identification 
certificate which ultimately caused has forced idleness. A 
dispute hat, been raised before the A.L.C. (C), Dhanbad on 
7-4-89 against the stoppage from duty. He is entitled to 
receive wages from 10-11-87 to 6-4-89 under the Payment 
of Wages AG, 1936 which, as per computation comes to 
Rs, 23,851.51. 

3. The Opp. Party—management contested the matter by 
submitting written ’>T.itcment-cum-rejoindcr. The written 
stalcment-eum-rcjoinder discloses that the present applica¬ 
tion is not maintainable. The applicant assumed the name 
of Radheyshyom, a delisted casual wagon loader of Sendra 
Bamjora Colliery and entered into service of the manage¬ 
ment surreptitiously as Bud I i miner/loader in 1981 in pur¬ 
suance of circular dated 4-8-80 of the management. He was 
transferred from Sendra Bansjora Colliery to Loyabau Col¬ 
liery on 22-11-83 as miner/loadcr. It was reported to the 
management that the applicant was not a genuine wokman. 

• He was directed by several letters to produce the certificate 
of B.D.O. under whoso jurisdiction his village is situated so 
that he could he 'allowed to work. The applicant failed to 
produce any certificate of his genuinity. He i's not a genuine 
workman, his initial appointment was irregular and the 
management was justified in stopping him from duty. 

4. In rejoinder to the written statement of the manage¬ 
ment, the concerned applicant has stated that the action of 
the management in stopping him from work is the subject 
matter of industrial dispute and the same is pending before 
(he Ministry of Labour. He was on the roll of the colliery 
establishment and bis titoppage from work amounts to forced 
idleness. He is entitled to receive wages from the date of 
stoppage from 10-11-87. 

5 In rejoinder to the written statement of the applicant, 
the manngemenf has staled that stoppage of work of the 
applicant was not wrong or illegal. The workman miv be 
stopped from duty without issuing ebargesheet or holding 
cnouiry. if his appointment is irregular and illegal. The 
applicant is not a genuine workman. Calculations given in 
+ he annexnre are incorrect. 

6 . The 'applicant hai examined himself as WW-1 and laid 
in evidence one item of document, viz. p'ny slip which has 
hern marked Ext. W-l. 

The Opp. Party ha? not laid any evidence, either oral or 

documentary.

ANNEXURE 

BEFORE TIIE CENTRAL GOVERNMENT 1NDTJSTRJAL 
TRIBUNAL NO. I, DI-IANBAD 


[wt II—5m 3 (ii)] 


7. The applicant lias stated that he got appointment in 
Sendra Banxojru Colliery ol M/s. B.C.C. Ltd. us mtr.et. The 
management has not disputed tile fact that he got employ¬ 
ment in Sendra Bansjora Colliery. The applicant ha\ suited 
that he was transferred to L.oyubad Colliery as Prop Muzdoor 
TJie management has also stated in its written xt'aKmrnt 
that he was raitsferred from Sendra Buntijojra Colliery to 
Eoybnd Colliery on 22-11 -E 3 as miner/loader. 

8. The case of the Opp. Party is that the applicant is not 
a genuine workman and he was asked to produce the certi¬ 
ficate of genuinity from the local B.D.O. The applicant has 
stated in his testimony that he was granted ten days time to. 
produce certificac and ho could produce the same after etc 
Party has not dispensed with the service of the applicant, 
stopped him from service. Anyway, the management has 
stopped him from service with effect from 10-11-87, 

9. It Has been contended by the management that it has 
right to ‘stop him from duty as the initial appointment of 
the applicant is illegal. But this is a matter of evidence, 
the management has not laid any whit of evidence, to prove 
this fact. By the gption of the management—Ono. Party, 
the applicant was subjected to force idleness. The Opp. 
Party has not dispensed with the service of the applicant. 
This being so, the applicant has remained in the employ¬ 
ment of the munagement—Opp. Party as before and the 
management—Opp. Party is responsible for payment of all 
wages to the applicant as he continued to remain employed 
by it. 

10. The applicant has computed his wages for the period 
from 10-11-87 to 6-4-89 which includes Basic, Attendance 
Bonus, Spl. D.A., F.D.A. and V.D.A. at Rs, 23,851.51. The 
pay slip produced b v him fExt. W-1) supports his claim in 
some material respects. The applicant has not been cross- 
examined on the point and his cross-examination docs no 1 
reveal that his computation is any way faulted. This being 
the position, T allow the entire claim of the applicant. 

11. Hence ordered—that the L.C. case be disposed of on 
contest. The applicant is entitled t 0 recover n um of 
Rs. 23,851.51 from the Opp. party—management, The Opp. 
Party—management Is directed to pav the amount to the 
applicant within two mnnhs from the date of notification of 
the order.

BEFORE SHRI ARVTND KUMAR, PRESIDING OFFICER, 

CENTRAL GOVERNMENT INDUSTRIAL, TR1BUNAI - 
CUM-LABOUR COURT, CHANDIGARH 

Case No. 122/89 

Ram Chander Vs. Telecommunication. 

For the workman : Shri H. ,S. Bath. 

For the management: Shri Arun Walia. 

AWARD 

Central Government vide Garotte Notification No. L-40012/ 
78/88/D.2.(B), dated 1-8-1989 issued 11 /<,. 10(1 ltd) of the 
I.D. Act, 1947 referred the following dispute to this Tribunal 
for adjudication : 

“Whether the action of I he Telecommunication Distt. 
Kngg., Karnal in terminating the setvices of Shri 
Ram Chander S/o Sadhu Ram. casual worker w.e.f. 
1-8-87 and again on 1-8-1988 is just, fair and legal'.’ 
If not, to what relief the worker concerned is entitled 
to ?" 

2. In the statement of claim it has been alleged that the 
petitioner has put in more than one year of service with 
the respondent management as mnzdoor to the satisfaction 
of the respondent. It is further alleged that his appointment 
was through employment exchange and there was no hi cab¬ 
in the service. He has further alleged that his services were 
illegally terminated on 1-8-1987 without any legal notice, 
without payment of retrenchment compensation alongwith ie- 
trenchment order which is condition precedent and thus the 
respondent management has violated the provisions of section 
25-F of the I.D. Act. 1947. It is further alleged that the 
respondent management has violated the provisions of section 
25-F and H of the I.D. Act. 19t7 as juniors namely Adilya 
Prashud and Dharnmvir have been retained in the service. 
It is further alleged that there is lot of work with the tele¬ 
communication department and there is no need to reliench 
the workman petitioner, ft is also alleged that he lias put 
in more than 240 days set vice with the respondent manage¬ 
ment and prayed that he he reinstated with full hack wages 
and continuity of scivice. 

U The clnun of the petitioner was contested. It has km- 
pleaded that the services of the petitioner was terminated 
w.e.f, 1-8-87. It was admitted that the retrenchment com¬ 
pensation w-ns given on 9-10-87 and thus provisions of Sec 
lion 25-E of the T.D. Act. 19-17 has been complied. Other 
contentions were denied. Tt is further pleaded that no inniors 
been retained nej thus there i\ no violation of Srcf’on 
25-F and H and prayed for dismissal of the reference. 

4. Replication was also filed reasserting the claim made ip 
the claim petition. 

'’71 GI/93—10 


5. The petitioner in support of his-case .produced himself 
as WW1 and filed his affidavit Ex. W1 in evidence and also 
relied on documents F.x. W2 to W4 the circulars. Respondent 
management produced Shri G. S. Bains MW! who tender 
his affidavit Ex. Ml and relied on the documents Fxs. M2 
to M3 showing payment of retrenchment compensation and 
Fxs. M4 lo M5 the documents showing the number of days 
put in hy the workman. In cross-exami'ation he has admitted 
that no notice issued lo the workman retrenching him, no 
reason was given, He has also admitted in cross-examination 
that alongwith the said notice no ictrcnehment compensation 
was given lo the workman. 

6. I have heard both the p.n'ics and gone through the 
evidence on the record. I earned Counsel appearing on be¬ 
half of workman has argued that the respondent management 
has violated the provisions of Scclion 25-F of the I.D. Act, 
1947 as the workman was not paid retrenchment compensa¬ 
tion alongwith retrenchment order which is condition prece¬ 
dent although the petitioner has completed 240 days in the 
preceding year to the date of retrenchment. I find force in 
this contention. Services of the petitioner were terminated 
w.e.f. 1-8-87. lx. M4 and F.x. M5 are the muster rolls show¬ 
ing the number of days pul in by (he petitioner In the pre- 
i' 1 din" year *o 'he (file o f retrenchment. The petitioner has 
completed the service of 240 days continuously in the preced¬ 
ing venr lo the date of retrenchment. Clause ‘B* of Section 
?s-T lavs down that as a precondition to retrenenment as 
the workman should be paid compensation at the time of 
retrenchment. This condition is a condition precedent to a 
valid order of retrenchment. If. therefore no retrenchment 
compensation is paid lo the workman before they are asked 
to go. retrenchment order is had invalid and inoperative in 
law-. If. retrenchment order is invalid, an-initio, subsequent 
payment of compensalion cannot validate it. From the docu¬ 
ments F\s M2 to M3 it is appeicnt that the retrenchment 
compensation was offered on'v on 9-10-1987 although the 
termination was effected w.e.f. 1-8-87. Not only this the 
management's witness Shri G. S. Bains MW1 has also admit¬ 
ted to this effect. If any authority is needed 1970(2) E.E.I. 
page 179 Somu Kumar Clmlleriee Vs. District Signal Tele¬ 
communication Fnttineer and 1967(23 E.I. T. page 23 National 
Iron and Steel Co. Vs. Stale of West Bengal can be read 
in this regard. 

In view of the discussion made in ihe earlier paras, the 
termination of the petitioner is certainly had in the eye of 
law and is set aside. He is ordered to hr reinstated with 
continuity of service with all consequential benefits. 

However, with regard to the hack wages, the netitioner in 
the statement of claim has nowhere stated that he remained 
unemployed through out nor such stand has been taken dur¬ 
ing the course of evidence. Tn all fairness the petitioner is 
awarded onlv 50 per cent of the back wages ihnt too w.e.f. 
the date of reference i.e. 1-R-19R9. 

Announced. Subject to the approval 
hv the Ministry of Labour. 

3-12-1992.

w.e.f. 1-8-87. Ex. M4 und Ex, M5 are the must rolls show¬ 
ing the number of days put in by the petitioner in the pre¬ 
ceding year to Ihe date of retrenchment. The petitioner has 
compleied Ihe service of 240 day continuousy_ in the prece¬ 
ding year to the date of retrenchment. Clause ‘R’ of Sec¬ 
tion 25-F lays down that as a precondition to retrenchment 
as the workman should he paid compensation at the time of 
retrenchment. This condition is a condition precedent to a 
valid order of retrenchment. If, therefore no retrenchment 
compensation is naid to the workman before ihey are asked 
to go, retrenchment order is had, invalid and inoperative in 
law. If retrenchment order is invalid, ohinitto, subsequent 
payment of compensation can not validate it From the docu¬ 
ments Ex. M2. M3 it is nnparent that the retrenchment 
compensation was offered only on 4-10-87 although the ter¬ 
mination was effected w.e.f. 1 8-87. Not only this the man¬ 
agement's witness Shri G. S. Rains MW1 has also admitted 
to this effect. Tf anv authority is needed 1970(2) L.T..L pfl|je 
179 Somn Kumar Chatteriee Vs. District Signal Telecommu¬ 
nication Engineer and 19870 T page 23 National Iron 
and ?*eel Co. Vs. State of West Bengal can be vend in this 
regard. 

In view of the discussion made in the earlier paras, the 
termination of the netirinuer is certainly had in the eye of 
law and ir, set aside. He is ordered to be reinstated with 
continuity of service with all consequential benefits.

3 Tltc claim of the petitioner Was contested. It 
has been pleaded that the services of the petitioner 
was terminated vv.c.f. 1-8-1987, It was ad- 
■ni'n.ed thni the retrenchment- compensation was

merK compensation was given to the workman¬ 
te l have heard both die partie; and gone through 

he evidence on the record- Learned counsel ap¬

w.e.f. 1-8-1987. Ex. M4 and Ex. M5 are the must

retrenchment. This condition is a condition prece¬ 
dent to a valid order of retrenchment If, therefore, 
no retrenchment compensation is paid to the workman 
before they are asked to go retrenchment order is bad, 
invalid and inoperative in law. If, retrenchment 
order is invalid, abinitio. subsequent payment of com¬ 
pensation can not validate it. From the documents 
Ex. M2 to M3 it is apparent that ,he retrenchment 
compensation was offered only on 9-10-1987 although

only this the management's witness Shri G.S. Bains 
MWl has also admit: ed to this effect. If any autho¬ 
rity is needed 1970(2) L.L.J. page 179 Somu Kumar

rega rd.

his cabin at the top of her voice. MW5 is the Ac¬ 
countant attached to that branch and he also de¬ 
posed that he has seen the worker abusing the Senior 
Manager. MW 12 is a Clerk working in that branch 
and she has also deposed that she has seen the work- 
ker abusing the Senior Manager. Thus it can be 
seen that with regard to this incident these witnesses 
hav e given a more-or-less identifical and uniform 
version. On going through this version 1 do not find 
any discrepancy or inconsistency. Further it has 
come out that these witnesses have no axe to grind 
against. this worker. So the question naturally arises 
whv these witnes c es who hav 0 absolutely no enimity 
to this worker gave such an evidence against her. 
Thus as richtlv pointed out by the Enquiry Officer 
the testimonv of these witnesses prove beyond doubt 
that, thf worker has abused the Sen'or Manager op 
22-6-1988 as alEged in the first charge. So much 
so the finding of the Enauiry Officer holding the 
worker guilty of the first charge can be upheld. 

9. Now coming to thc second charge, the said 
charge is that the worker abused the Branch Mana¬ 
ger o' ffie Cherootty Road Branch on 12-7-1988. 
Th r . Senior Manager who tendered oral evidence as 
MWl has also spoken with regard to this charge but 
his testimony with resnect to this cWtm does not 
have much of a relevancy here since hp has deposed 
only that with regard to this incident he got a report 
from thc sa : d Branch Manager. The Branch Manager 
who is said to have been thus abused bv the worker 
has been ^vsmlipd as MW4 and he has denoted 
that on 12-7-1988 the worker came to him and told 
h : m that she could not reach the bank in time as she 
did not not bos and then be told her that she does 
not have to clean the floor on that day as the staff 
ha^e nW-»dv taken their seats and then the worker 
271 GI/93—12 


started abusing him in filthy language. This versioa 
was corroborated by the testimonies of MW6 and 
MW 11 who are the clerical staffs working in that 
branch. They hav e also deposed that on 12-7-1988 
the worker has abused at the top of her voice the 
Branch Manager and created an ugly scene in the 
the branch. MW 15 is the another worker working 
in that branch and he has also deposed that the 
worker has abused the branch Manager. I see no 
reason at all to disbelieve the testimonies of these 
witnesses sine© on going through it, it seems to mo 
that they have only spoken the truth. Here also the 
worker has absolutely no case that these witnesses are 
at logger-heads with her. So the possibility of these 
Witnesses giving false evidence against the worker 
can be ruled out. What the Enquiry Officer has 
done is that he believed thc testimonies of these 
witnesses to hold the worker guility of this 
charge. I too feel that these witnesses have only 
narrated what they have actually seen in the Branch 
Manager’s room on 12-7-1988, and hence their testi¬ 
monies can be fully believed. I therefore conclude 
that the Enquiry officer is right in holding the wor¬ 
ker guilty of thc second charge also relying on the 
testimonies of MW4, MW6, MW 11 and MW 15. 

10. This point is thus decided in favour of the 
management holding that the finding of the Enquiry 
Officer holdig the worker guilty of the two charges 
is supported by legal evidence and that it is sus¬ 
tainable. 

11. Point No. 3 :—Lastly comes the question of 
punishment. The counsel for the management sought 
to justify the imposition of extreme penalty of dis¬ 
missal stating that on previous two occasions the 
worker was punished for similar misconducts by 
stopping her yearly increments. But even then she 
did not improve and hence a deterrent punishment 
is called for. The fact that the worker was punched 
previously on two occasions for similar misconducts 
is not disputed by the worker and this fact is more- 
or-less concerned by her in her claim statement. 
Thus it can be seen that though she was subieefed 
to disdiolmarv proceedings twice earner for similar' 
misconducts she, has not improved. This shows that 
she is incorrig'ble and this being her nature the 
management is fully justified in dispensing with her 
service as punishment. But any wav the worker be ; ng 
a lady in her fifty’s who has put in around 15 years 
of service I think firs dispensation of serving woffid 
have been in a mi'der wav bv giving her compulsory 
retirement, T therefore convert the punishment of 
d’snvssnl given to hm - by the management into that 
of compulsory retirement. 

12 In the result an award is passed altering the 
punishment of dismissal given to the worker into 
that of ■'oniplusorv retirement. 

13. This award w‘11 mnw into force 30 days afte? 
its pub 1 ication in the Official Gazette,

bv him, revised, corrected and passed by me on the 
4th day of January, 1993.

New Delhi, the 21st January, 1993 

S.O. 321.—In exercise of the powers conferred by 
Section 87 read with section 91-A of the Employees’ 
State Insurance Act, 1948 (34 of 1948), (he Central 
Government hereby exempts M|s. Hindustan Antibio¬ 
tics Limited, Pimpri, Pune from the operation of the 
said Act for a period of one year with effect from 1st 


October, 91 upto and inclusive of the 30th Septem¬ 
ber, 1992. 

2 . Tire above exemption is subject to the following 
conditions, namely :— 

(1) The employer of the said factory shall submit 

in respect of the period during which that 
factory was submit to the operation of the 
said Act (hereinafter referred to as the said 
period) such returns in such form and con¬ 
taining such particulars as were due from it 
in respect of the said period under the Emp¬ 
loyees’ State Insurance (General) Regula¬ 
tions, 1950. 

(2) Any Inspector appointed by the Corporation 

under sub-section (1) of Section 43 of said 
Act or other official of the Corporation au¬ 
thorised in this behalf shall, for the pur¬ 
pose of :— 

(i) verifying the particulars contained in any 

return submitted under sub-section (1) of 
section 44 for the said period; or 

(ii) ascertaining whether registers and records 

were maintained as required by ;he Emp¬ 
loyees’ State Insurance (General) Regula¬ 
tions, 1950 for the said period; or 

(iii) ascertaining whether the employees continue 

to be entitled to benefits provided by the 
employer in cash and kind being benefits 
in consideration which exemption is’ being 
granted under this notification; or 

(iv) ascertaining whether any of the provisions of 

the sa ; d Act W: been complied with during 
the period when such provisions were i" 
force in relation to the said factory ; 

be empowered to :— 

(a) reouirc the principal or immediate employer 
to furnish to him such information as he may 
consider necessary; or 

(b) enter any factory, establishment, office of 
other premises occupied by such principal or 
immediate employer at any reasonable time 
and require any person found inchargc there¬ 
of to produce to such Inspector or other 
official and allow him to examine such ac¬ 
counts books and other documents relafing 
to the employment of persons and payment 
of wages or to furnish to him such informa¬ 
tion as be may consider necessary; or 

(c) examine the principal or immediate employer, 

his agent or servant or any person found ini 
such factory, eS ablishment, office or other 
premises or any person whom the said Ins¬ 
pector or other official has reasonable cause 
to believe to have been an employee; or 

(d) make copies of or take extracts from any re¬ 
gister, account book or other document main¬ 
tained in such factory, establishment, office 
or other premises. 

(F. No. S-38014| 39191-SST) 
J. P. SHUKLA, Under Secy.
